<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy - Intro</summary>

**Book
V : Sundara Kanda - Book Of Beauty**

This first chapter of Sundara Kanda describes Hanuma's preparations to cross the
ocean in search of Seetha. Mighty Hanuma's flight over the ocean is described in a glorious detail.
Hanuma's encountering of Mainaka, Surasa and Simhika form important phases of His flight.
</details>

<details><summary>भागसूचना</summary>

1. हनुमान् जी के द्वारा समुद्रका लङ्घन, मैनाकके द्वारा उनका स्वागत, सुरसापर उनकी विजय तथा सिंहिकाका वध करके उनका समुद्रके उस पार पहुँचकर लंकाकी शोभा देखना
</details>
</details>

Resolved to trace Sītā abducted by Rāvaṇa along the sky-path of the Cāraṇas, mighty Hanumān strides like a bull and a lion across Mount Mahendra’s mineral-rich slopes.
</details>
</details>

तदनन्तर शत्रुओंका संहार करनेवाले हनुमान् जी ने रावणद्वारा हरी गयी सीताके निवासस्थानका पता लगानेके लिये उस आकाशमार्गसे जानेका विचार किया, जिसपर चारण (देवजातिविशेष) विचरा करते हैं ॥ १ ॥
</details>

शत्रुसंहारकनाद हनुमन्तनु (जाम्बवन्तर प्रेरणॆयिन्द) अद्भुतवाद शक्तियन्नु स्मरिसिकॊण्डु रावणनिन्द अपहृतळाद सीतॆय जाडन्नु हुडुकुव सलुवागि, चारणादि देवतॆगळु सञ्चरिसुव आकाश मार्गदिन्द प्रयाण माडलु बयसिदनु.॥1॥

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*1. tataH* = after that; *shatrukarshanaH* = the destroyer of
foes (Hanuma); *iyeshha* = desired to (travel in); *pathi* = the path; *chaaraNaa
charite* = tread by chaaranas; *anveshhTum* = to search for; *siithayaaH padam* =
Seetha's place; *raavaNa niitaayaaH* = taken away by Ravana.

**अनुवादः**

After that, Hanuma, the destroyer of foes, desired to travel in the sky where
celestials like chaaranas tread, to search for Seetha, who was taken away by Ravana.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Then that tormentor of foes (_śatrukarṣaṇaḥ_) wished to seek out the track (_padam_) of Sītā, who had been carried off by Rāvaṇa, along the sky-path frequented by Cāraṇas (_cāraṇācarite pathi_).
</details>


<details><summary>टीका-notes (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

**1. Opening Exegesis of the Sundarakāṇḍa (5.1.1)**

Following Jāmbavān’s exhortation (जाम्बवत्प्रोत्साहनानन्तरं), Hanumān prepares to leap across the sea. **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) and **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) gloss पथि as the sky-path (आकाशमार्गे) traversed by celestial Cāraṇas. **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) offers both a literal reading (अन्तरिक्षचारिणो देवगायकाः) and a Śrīvaiṣṇava allegory in which Hanumān exemplifies the spiritual preceptor (आचार्यकृत्यमुच्यते) walking the path of ancient teachers (पूर्वाचार्याः) to seek the captive soul (सीतायाः), noting that this verse inaugurates the epic’s twelfth thousand (द्वादशसहस्रस्यादिमोऽयं श्लोकः).

_____________

**1. The Lake-Elephant Simile and Travel Prayer (5.1.7–8)**

Completing the syntactic unit of verses 5–7, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) notes that the mountain resembles a deep lake through its dark sheen (गिरौ ह्रदसाम्यं श्यामलत्वेन), while **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) glosses both नाग terms as elephants (नागो गज इव). On **5.1.8**, **Rāma Varmā** remarks that Hanumān’s salutation teaches the traditional code of conduct (सदाचारो बोधितः) that a journey must begin with prayers to one’s tutelary deities to remove all obstacles, while **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) glosses स्वयम्भुवे as four-faced Brahmā (चतुर्मुखाय) and भूतेभ्यः as divine beings (देवयोनिभ्यः).

_____________

**1. The Flower-Mantled Peak and Mineral Streaks (5.1.13–15)**

On **5.1.13**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) explain पुष्पमयः with the suffix _-mayaṭ_ of abundance (प्रचुरपुष्पवानिव बभौ). On **5.1.14–15**, crushed by Hanumān, Mount Mahendra sweats water like an elephant in musth (मदमत्तो द्विप इव) and extrudes metallic veins (रीतीः). **Rāma Varmā** glosses रीतीः as flowing stream-streaks (स्रोतसः प्रकारान्) gleaming like gold, antimony, and silver, while **Śivasahāya** and **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) take रीतीः as mineral lines (रेखाः) or oozing fissures (भेदधाराः) bursting from the splitting rocks.

_____________

**1. Hood-Markings and Venom-Shattered Boulders (5.1.19–21)**

On **5.1.19**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) explain the स्वस्तिक as the dark line on a serpent’s hood (फणास्थनीलरेखा), whereas **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) identifies it as the half-moon mark visible atop the hood (फणोपरि दृश्यमानार्धचन्द्रकं) and notes that biting rocks is an expression of rage (दंशनं कोपव्यापारः). On **5.1.20–21**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Govindarāja** gloss बिभिदुः as split apart (भिन्ना बभूवुः) and शमितुं as causative in sense (शमयितुम्, 'to quell'), as even the mountain’s antivenom herb-clusters (औषधसमूहाः) fail against the Nāgas' fiery venom.

_____________

**1. The Wine-Flushed Vidyādharas and Their Consorts (5.1.25–26)**

Completing the three-verse unit (श्लोकत्रयमेकान्वयि in _Śiromaṇi_, 23–25), the fleeing Vidyādharas reach the sky. All three commentators gloss कण्ठगुणाः as necklaces or garlands (स्रजः) and क्षीबाः as intoxicated (मत्ताः). **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) distinguishes रक्ताक्षाः ('red-eyed from drinking wine', मधुपानात्) from पुष्कराक्षाः ('naturally lotus-eyed', स्वभावतः). On **5.1.26**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** cite Kataka taking पारिहार्य as 'excellent' (श्रेष्ठवाची) and Tīrtha taking it as a bracelet (वलयः, followed by **Govindarāja**).

_____________

**1. The Vidyādharas’ Realization and the Garuḍa Similes (5.1.31–34)**

On **5.1.31**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) remarks that before hearing the celestial sages’ words (**5.1.29–30**), the Vidyādharas had supposed the mountain was being shattered by elemental spirits (भूतैरयं गिरिर्भिद्यत इति ज्ञातवन्तः); only now do they behold Hanumān as the cause. On **5.1.33–34**, **Rāma Varmā** glosses आनुपूर्व्या वृत्तं as coiled in three rings (त्रिवलयं) and आविद्धं as lashed and curved (आस्फोटितं भुग्नं च), while **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) and **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) take आनुपूर्व्या वृत्तं as tapering roundly in due sequence (क्रमशः वृत्तं वर्तुलं), likening the brandished tail behind his back to a great serpent carried off by Garuḍa.

_____________

**1. Breath Control and Launch Stance (5.1.37–38)**

On **5.1.37**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) glosses हृदये as the seat where the vital breaths abide (प्राणप्रतिष्ठास्थाने), while **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) explains that restraining the breath (प्राणनिरोधः) is practiced specifically for leaping into the sky (व्योमोत्पतनार्थम्). On **5.1.38**, **Rāma Varmā** notes that contracting the ears (कर्णसंकोचः) naturally accompanies the tensing of the body for a leap (उत्पतनसंनाहवशात्), and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) observes that verses 38–39a form a single syntactic unit.

_____________

**1. The Effortless Leap and Garuḍa Self-Conception (5.1.43–44)**

On **5.1.43**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) compares Hanumān’s vow to uproot Laṅkā to his later feat of uprooting the herb-mountain (औषधिपर्वतोत्पाटनवत्). On **5.1.44**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) gloss अविचारयन् as reckoning the leap across the ocean to involve no fatigue whatsoever (उल्लङ्घनक्लेशमप्यगणयन् / किंचिदपि श्रमजनकमित्यगणयन्), which the second half-verse elaborates through his deeming himself equal to Garuḍa (सुपर्णमिव).

_____________

**1. Heartwood Trees and the Myth of Winged Mountains (5.1.49–50)**

On **5.1.49**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) explains पर्वताकारः as resembling a mountain both by his vast bulk and by bearing flowering trees (तत्समबृहत्त्वेन पुष्पितवृक्षवत्त्वेन च). On **5.1.50**, all three commentators gloss सारवन्तः as trees heavy with dense heartwood (गुरुत्वातिशययुक्ताः खदिरादयः in _Tilaka_; citing the lexicon सारो बले स्थिरांशे च in _Śiromaṇi_ and _Bhūṣaṇa_), which plunge immediately into the sea just as the ancient mountains hid in the ocean from fear of Indra’s wing-clipping thunderbolt (पक्षच्छेदभयात्), implying that lighter trees travelled further before falling.

_____________

**1. Five-Hooded Serpents and Drinking Ocean and Sky (5.1.55–57)**

On **5.1.55**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) glosses रामाभिः as lovely (रमणीयाभिः). After comparing Hanumān’s two outstretched five-fingered arms to five-hooded serpents darting from a mountain peak (**5.1.56**), Vālmīki depicts him as drinking the sea and sky (**5.1.57**). **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) explain that with his mouth gaping wide (विवृतवक्त्रत्वात्), whenever he looks down (अधोवक्त्रत्वे) he seems to drink the ocean, and whenever he looks up (ऊर्ध्वमुखत्वे) he seems to thirst for the sky; **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) adds that his sheer speed makes the receding expanse of sea and sky appear as though being swallowed up (पीड्यमान इव भवति).

_____________

**1. Indra’s Banner, the Solar Halo, and Red Chalk Ore (5.1.61–63)**

On **5.1.61–62**, all three commentators gloss समाविद्धं as raised and curved (उन्नतीकृतं), comparing the upright tail to Indra’s festival banner (शक्रध्वजमिव) and its circular coil around Hanumān to a halo around the sun (परिवेषी मण्डलवान् भास्कर इव); **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) notes that शुक्लदंष्ट्रः is descriptive of his natural form (स्वरूपकथनमात्रम्) rather than part of the halo simile. On **5.1.63**, **Rāma Varmā** explains that because only his hip-region (कटिप्रदेशेन) is bright coppery red, the rest of the mountain-like monkey resembles a peak split open to reveal a single great seam of red ochre (दारितगैरिकधातुयुक्तगिरिणापमा).

_____________

**1. The Wind-Driven Ship and the Frenzied Sea (5.1.67–68)**

On **5.1.67**, all three commentators admire the double image of Hanumān’s body coursing through the sky above (उपरिष्टात्) while his reflection or shadow plunges into the sea below (अवगाढया छायया): **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) explains that just as a ship has its hull submerged in water and its upper deck in the air, so Hanumān’s sky-borne body and water-plunged shadow form a single ship-like figure (एकाकारः सन्) driven by the wind (मारुताविष्टा नौरिवासीत्). On **5.1.68**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) explain सोन्माद इव ('like one possessed by epilepsy/madness', सापस्मार इव) through the shared symptoms of whirling, foaming, and roaring (घूर्णनफेनवमनविक्रोशनादिविकारसाम्यात्).

_____________

**1. Sky-Borne Spray and Unclothed Sea Creatures (5.1.73–74)**

On **5.1.73**, **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_), reading वेगसमुद्धूतं, explains that the gale of Hanumān’s thighs flings ocean water up into the clouds (ऊरुवातेन समुत्थापितं सजलदं जलं), where the mingled spray and cloud shine like an expansive white autumn cloud (शारदाभ्रमिवाततम्). On **5.1.74**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_), **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_), and **Govindarāja** explain विवृताः as exposed when the water covering them is swept aside by his blast (वेगाज्जलोत्सारणेन व्यक्तदेहाः / जलरूपप्रावरणरहिताः), comparing the bared whales (तिमयो महामत्स्याः), crocodiles (नक्राः ग्राहाः), and turtles to human bodies stripped of their garments (वस्त्रापकर्षणेन).

_____________

**1. The Furrowed Ocean and the Bird-Path (5.1.79–80)**

On **5.1.79**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) gloss द्रोणी as a wooden roof-gutter or water-channel (हर्म्यादौ जलनिर्गमकाष्ठयन्त्रं), while **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) glosses it as a basin or cauldron (कटाहः), describing the deep trough carved in the sea by Hanumān’s blast. On **5.1.80**, all three commentators derive आपाते in the sense of locus as the sky-track along which birds fly (आपतन्त्यस्मिन्नित्यापातो मार्गः), where Hanumān speeds like Garuḍa and drags cloud-banks like his father Vāyu.

_____________

**1. Universal Acclaim for the Tireless Emissary (5.1.85–86)**

Watching Hanumān bound across the sky without fatigue (विगतक्लमम्) despite performing a feat impossible for others (अन्यासाध्यव्यापारवत्त्वेऽपि in _Tilaka_), sages, gods, Gandharvas, Nāgas, and Yakṣas praise him. On **5.1.86**, **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) explains that the Rākṣasas who join in praising Hanumān are not Rāvaṇa’s partisans, but the attendants of Kubera, regent of the northern quarter (दिक्पालकुबेरसंबन्धीनि).

_____________

**1. Golden-Peaked Maināka as the Bolt of Pātāla (5.1.91–93)**

On **5.1.91**, **Rāma Varmā** (_Tilaka_) and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) cite the lexicon नाभिः प्रधाने कस्तूर्याम् to gloss हिरण्यनाभं as 'predominantly golden' (हिरण्यप्रधानम्), whereas **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) cites नाभिरध्यक्षे कस्तूर्याम् to gloss it as 'having a golden peak' (हिरण्यशृङ्गं, since a peak is the chief part of a mountain). On **5.1.92–93**, **Rāma Varmā** explains that after Maināka hid in the sea out of fear of Indra’s wing-clipping, Indra stationed him like a door-bar (परिघवत्) at the subterranean exit to block the Asuras of Pātāla, whose valor Indra already knew (ज्ञातवीर्याणां देवराजेनेति शेषः).

_____________

**1. The Ethics of Hospitality and Duty (5.1.97–99)**

Continuing the Ocean’s exhortation, **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) glosses कार्यं as the pressing duty of providing rest (विश्रान्त्यादि), warning that neglecting an obligatory duty provokes the righteous anger (मन्युं कोपम्) of the good (**5.1.97**). Addressing Maināka as चामीकरमहानाभ ('possessing great peaks of gold', स्वर्णमयमहाशृङ्ग, **5.1.99**), the Ocean invokes the sacred status of a guest (अतिथिः, **5.1.98**).
</details>
</details>

दुष्करं निष्प्रतिद्वन्द्वं चिकीर्षन् कर्म वानरः ।  
समुदग्रशिरोग्रीवो गवां पतिरिवाबभौ ॥ २ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

दुष्करं निष्प्रतिद्वन्द्वं चिकीर्षन् कर्म वानरः ।  
समुदग्रशिरोग्रीवो गवां पतिरिवाबभौ ॥ २ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

कपिवर हनुमान् जी  ऐसा कर्म करना चाहते थे, जो दूसरोंके लिये दुष्कर था तथा उस कार्यमें उन्हें किसी और की सहायता भी नहीं प्राप्त थी । उन्होंने मस्तक और ग्रीवा ऊँची की । उस समय वे हृष्ट-पुष्ट साँड़के समान प्रतीत होने लगे ॥ २ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हनुमन्तनु साटिये इल्लद, अत्यन्त दुष्करवाद कार्यवन्नु माडलु निर्धरिसि, तलॆ मत्तु कुत्तिगॆयन्नु मेलक्कॆत्ति सॆटॆदुनिन्तिद्द अवनु गूळिगळ राजनन्तॆ विराजिसुत्तिद्दनु.॥2॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*2. vaanaraH* = The monkey (Hanuma); *chikiirshhan* = who
desired to perform; *karma* = a deed; *dushhkaram* = (which is) impossible to do;
*babhau* = shone; *gavaam pati iva* = like a bull; *nishhpratidvandvam* =
without any obstacles; *samudagrashirogriivaH* = with a raised head and neck.

**अनुवादः**

Hanuma who desired to perform a deed done by no one else, shone like a bull without
any obstacles, with his stretched neck and raised head.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Desiring to perform (_cikīrṣan_) a feat hard to accomplish and peerless (_niṣpratidvandvam_), the Vānara, with his head and neck held high (_samudagraśirogrīvaḥ_), shone like a lord of cattle [a bull] (_gavāṃ patir iva_).
</details>


<details><summary>टीका-notes (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

**2. The Peerless Feat and Heroic Similes (5.1.2–4)**

On **5.1.2**, **Rāma Varmā** explains निष्प्रतिद्वन्द्वं as devoid of any companion or obstacle (सहायान्तररहितं प्रतिबन्धरहितं), while **Śivasahāya** glosses it as having no rival doer (द्वितीयः कर्तापि वा यस्य); all three commentators take गवां पतिः as a bull (वृषभः). **Śivasahāya** notes that **5.1.3–4** form a single syntactic unit (श्लोकद्वयमेकान्वयि): the cat’s-eye meadows (शाद्वलेषु) resemble ocean water (सागरजलसदृशेषु), where Hanumān roams like a full-grown lion (केसरी सिंह इव), scattering birds (द्विजान् पक्षिणः).

_____________

**2. Syntactic Debate on Facing East and Growing (5.1.9–10)**

On **5.1.9**, all three commentators gloss दक्षिणः as skilful or capable (कुशलः / सर्वकार्यदक्षः / समर्थः) and आत्मयोनये as his sire Vāyu. **Govindarāja** critiques the reading अञ्जलिं प्राङ्मुखः कुर्वन्, arguing that the present participle (शतृप्रत्ययेन) would make facing east simultaneous with setting out south (विरोधात्), and therefore supplies कृत्वा ('having made'). On **5.1.10**, **Rāma Varmā** glosses रामवृद्ध्यर्थं as for Rāma’s triumph (रामाभ्युदयाय).

_____________

**2. Realgar Boulders and the Middle Flame (5.1.16)**

All three commentators gloss समनःशिलाः as boulders containing realgar ore (मनःशिलासहिताः / धातुविशेषसहिताः). On मध्यमेनार्चिषा जुष्टः, **Rāma Varmā** notes two readings: taking धूमराजिरिव in the singular with an supplied genitive (यस्येत्यध्याहारः), or following Tīrtha (Maheśvaratīrtha), who reads accusative plural धूमराजीरिव ('masses of smoke', धूमनिचयानिव) and takes मध्यमेन as Agni’s tongue named Madhyamā. **Govindarāja** explains that the middle flame, unlike the side flames, is what emits smoke (मध्यमया ज्वालया पार्श्वज्वाला हि न धूमनिर्वर्तिका).

_____________

**2. The Flight of Ascetics and Vidyādharas (5.1.22)**

Terrified by the quaking peak, ascetics and Vidyādharas leap skyward with their women. **Rāma Varmā** glosses भूतैः as great elemental spirits (मरुत्प्रभृतिमहाभूतैः), while **Śivasahāya** explains it as powerful divine beings pervading earth, water, and fire (पृथिव्यापस्तेजःप्रभृतिदेवविशेषैः).

_____________

**2. The Mystic Lore and Compound Analysis (5.1.27–28)**

**Rāma Varmā** notes that Kataka regards verses 27–28 as an interpolation (श्लोकद्वयं प्रक्षिप्तं क्वचिदिति कतकः). **Śivasahāya** explains महाविद्यां as the mystic power of standing unsupported in mid-air (निरवलम्बनस्थितिरूपां स्वशक्तिं), while **Govindarāja** takes it as the eight great yogic powers beginning with _aṇimā_ (अणिमाद्यष्टमहासिद्धिम्) and analyzes विद्याधरमहर्षयः not as a _dvandva_ (द्वन्द्वसमासो न युक्तः) but as an _upamita-samāsa_ ('chief Vidyādharas like great sages', विद्याधरश्रेष्ठाः) because **5.1.31** names only Vidyādharas as the listeners.

_____________

**2. The Crouching Posture Before the Leap (5.1.35)**

All three commentators analyze the physical mechanics of Hanumān’s launch posture (उत्पतनसंनाहाभिनयः). **Rāma Varmā** explains बाहू संस्तम्भयामास as planting both arms firmly atop the mountain (पर्वतोपरि दृढप्रतिष्ठानम्) and कट्यामाससाद as narrowing his waist (कायप्रसारणात् कृशोऽभूत्) while extending his chest and head upward and contracting his back and feet below. **Śivasahāya** and **Govindarāja** similarly gloss it as contracting his torso (शरीरं संचकोचेत्यर्थः).

_____________

**2. The Simile of Rāghava’s Arrow (5.1.39–41)**

All three commentators gloss श्वसनविक्रमः as having the velocity of the wind (पवनवेगः). On **5.1.40**, **Rāma Varmā** notes that तद्वद्गमिष्यामि implies not merely flying like Rāma’s arrow, but returning successful just like it (तद्वदेव गत्वा कृतकार्य आगमिष्यामीति सूचितम्). On **5.1.41**, **Śivasahāya** reads कृतश्रमः ('having exerted myself'), whereas **Govindarāja** reads अकृतश्रमः and glosses it as 'unwearied' (अप्राप्तश्रमः).

_____________

**2. Uprooted Trees and Excited Lapwings (5.1.45–46)**

On **5.1.45**, all three commentators construe समुत्पतति as a locative absolute and gloss नगरोहिणः as mountain trees (पर्वतस्थवृक्षाः) drawing in their boughs (विटपान्) as the suction of Hanumān’s launch pulls them skyward. On **5.1.46**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** explain कोयष्टिभकः and कोयष्टिकः as synonymous names for the lapwing water-bird (कोयष्टिकोयष्टिभौ पर्यायौ) nesting in the flowering treetops.

_____________

**2. Fireflies and Returning Friends (5.1.51–52)**

On **5.1.51**, **Śivasahāya** distinguishes अङ्कुरः ('unopened bud', कलिकात्वम्) from कोरकः ('blossoming bud', विकसितत्वम्), and **Govindarāja** supplies 'at night' (रात्राविति शेषः) to complete the simile of a dark mountain glowing with fireflies (खद्योतैः). On **5.1.52**, both **Rāma Varmā** and **Govindarāja** note the _ārṣa_ absence of the past augment _aṭ_ (आर्षोऽडभावः) in the reading अवशीर्यन्त, and compare the trees dropping into the waves after shedding their flowers to friends turning homeward (निवृत्ताः सुहृदो यथा) after escorting a departing kinsman.

_____________

**2. Forest Fires and the Twin Luminaries (5.1.58–59)**

On **5.1.58**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** gloss अनलौ as two mountain forest-fires (दावानलौ). On **5.1.59**, all three commentators explain पिङ्गाक्षमुख्यस्य as chief among tawny-eyed Vānaras (पिङ्गाक्षाणां वानराणां मुख्यस्य). **Rāma Varmā** notes that his two round eyes ringed with golden light (परिमण्डले) resemble the moon and the sun rising simultaneously atop the eastern mountain (उदयगिरावुदये), which **Govindarāja** classifies as an _abhūtopamā_ (a simile of an impossible phenomenon).

_____________

**2. The Thunder in His Armpits and the Tailed Meteor (5.1.64–65)**

On **5.1.64**, all three commentators take present गर्जति as past (अगर्जत्); **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) note that Kataka reads locative जीमूते ('in a cloud'), which **Śivasahāya** rejects for lack of decisive evidence (विनिगमनाविरहः). On **5.1.65**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** gloss उत्तरान्तात् as the upper northern sky (उत्तरोर्ध्वदिग्भागात्) and सानुबन्धा as a meteor with a trailing fiery tail (सपुच्छोल्का), matching Hanumān and his tail.

_____________

**2. The Partitive Genitive of the Waves (5.1.69)**

All three commentators gloss शैलवर्ष्मणाम् as having the bulk of mountains (पर्वताकाराणाम्), with **Govindarāja** citing Sajjana’s lexicon (वर्ष्म देहप्रमाणयोः). Both **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** explain the genitive plural ऊर्मिजालानाम् with अभिघ्नन् as a genitive expressing general relation in place of the direct object (कर्मणः शेषत्वविवक्षया षष्ठी), which **Rāma Varmā** notes implies striking only a fraction (एकदेशस्पर्शेन) of the countless ocean waves.

_____________

**2. Mistaken for Garuḍa by Sea-Serpents (5.1.75)**

Watching the giant monkey striding across the sky (व्योम्नि क्रममाणं), the serpents dwelling in the sea (सागरंगमाः भुजगाः) take him for their ancient foe Suparṇa (Garuḍa).

_____________

**2. Multi-Hued Clouds and the Moon Simile (5.1.81–82)**

Drawn in his wake, white, crimson, dark-blue, and madder-red clouds gleam across the sky (**5.1.81**), and as Hanumān plunges into and out of the cloud-banks (अभ्रजालानि), he resembles the moon alternately veiled and revealed (प्रच्छन्नश्च प्रकाशश्च चन्द्रमा इव, **5.1.82**).

_____________

**2. The Ocean’s Gratitude to the House of Ikṣvāku (5.1.87–88)**

All three commentators explain इक्ष्वाकुकुलमानार्थी by pointing to the etymology of सागरः ('Ocean'): because the sons of the Ikṣvāku monarch Sagara excavated and expanded his basin (स्वोत्पादकस्य / स्वजन्मकारणीभूतस्य), the Ocean seeks to honor their lineage (सत्कारमर्थयते). On **5.1.88**, **Rāma Varmā**, **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_), and **Govindarāja** gloss सर्ववाच्यः as 'universally blameworthy' (सर्वनिन्दास्पदं / सर्वैर्वक्तव्यो निन्दनीयः) among all speaking beings (विवक्षताम्).

_____________

**2. Growing Without Unbarring Pātāla (5.1.94–95)**

On **5.1.94**, **Rāma Varmā** explains why the Ocean mentions Maināka’s power to expand sideways, upward, and downward: Maināka can keep his base blocking Pātāla below while expanding upward to break the surface (अविहितपातालद्वार एव सन् वृद्धिशक्त्या वर्धमानः सन्नूर्ध्वमुत्तिष्ठ). On **5.1.95**, **Rāma Varmā** notes that Kataka regards the four and a half verses between खमाप्लुतः (**95b**) and श्रमं च (**100b**) as later interpolations (प्रक्षिप्ताः परैरिति कतकः).

_____________

**2. The Threefold Motive for Rising (5.1.100)**

In the verse-half retained even in Kataka’s shorter recension (श्रमं च प्लवगेन्द्रस्य समीक्ष्योत्थातुमर्हसि), the Ocean appeals to three motives: Rāma’s tender compassion toward Sītā (रामस्य सीताविषयां दयाम् in _Bhūṣaṇa_), Sītā’s cruel abduction and exile (मैथिल्याश्च विवासनम्), and Hanumān’s mid-ocean toil (श्रमं च).
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

फिर धीर स्वभाववाले वे महाबली पवनकुमार वैदूर्यमणि (नीलम) और समुद्रके जलकी भाँति हरी-हरी घासपर सुखपूर्वक विचरने लगे ॥ ३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

बळिक अन्तह धीरनाद, महाबलनाद हनुमन्तनु वैडूर्यदन्तॆ शोभिसुत्तिद्दु, मृदुवाद, स्वच्छवाद, विशाल जलराशिय वायुस्पर्शदिन्द एळुव तरङ्गगळिन्द शोभिसुत्तिरुव आ हच्चनॆय हुल्लुगावलिनल्लि, (मुन्दिन कार्यवन्नु योचिसुत्त) अत्तन्दित्त सञ्चरिसिदनु.॥3॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*3. atha* = after that; *mahaabalaH* = the mighty one;
*dhiiraH* = the courageous one; *vichachaara* = roamed about; *yathaa sukham*
= at ease; *shaadvaleshhu* = on the lawns; *vaiDuura varNeshhu* = with the hue of an
emerald; *salilakalpeshhu* = and same as (still) water (from a distance).

**अनुवादः**

After that, the mighty and courageous Hanuma roamed at ease on the lawns with the
hue of an emerald, which looked like still water from a distance.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Then across the cat’s-eye-colored (_vaidūryavarṇeṣu_) grassy meadows (_śādvaleṣu_) that resembled waters (_salilakalpeṣu_), the resolute hero of great might roamed at ease (_yathāsukham_).
</details>


<details><summary>टीका-notes (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

**3. Mineral Hues of Mount Mahendra (5.1.5–6)**

**Śivasahāya** observes that verses 5–7 form one syntactic unit (श्लोकत्रयमेकान्वयि) qualifying the mountain slope (तलं) in **5.1.7**. **Rāma Varmā** glosses नीललोहितः as dark-red (रक्तश्यामः) and सितासितैः as variegated black-and-white (कल्माषः कृष्णपाण्डुरिति यावत्). **Govindarāja**, reading पत्रवर्णैः, explains it as leaf-dark (पत्रश्यामैः) and glosses स्वभावविहितैः as naturally formed (स्वतःसिद्धैः) mineral ores.

_____________

**3. Pressing the Mountain (5.1.11–12)**

On **5.1.11**, **Rāma Varmā** glosses निष्प्रमाणशरीरः as having a body transcending limit (परिच्छेदातिक्रान्तदेहः), and **Govindarāja** explains that Hanumān presses with his arms as well (बाहुभ्यामित्युक्तिः) because monkeys move on all fours (वानरस्य चतुर्भिः संचारात्). On **5.1.12**, **Śivasahāya** glosses अशातयत् as caused to fall (अपातयदित्यर्थः).

_____________

**3. The Roar of Crushed Cave-Creatures (5.1.17–18)**

On **5.1.17**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** supply पर्वतेन with हरिणा पीड्यमानेन ('by the mountain being pressed by Hanumān'), whereas **Govindarāja** reads गिरिणा पीड्यमानेन directly and glosses विकृतैः as piteous (दैन्यव्यञ्जकैः). On **5.1.18**, **Rāma Varmā** cites both महासत्त्वसंनादः ('roar of mighty beasts') and महान् सत्त्वसंनादः.

_____________

**3. Abandoned Banqueting Vessels and Weapons (5.1.23–24)**

**Rāma Varmā** notes that the fleeing Vidyādharas are the subject of the gerund हित्वा ('abandoning', हित्येत्यत्र विद्याधराः कर्तारः), linking verses 22–24. **Śivasahāya** glosses आसवभाजनं as a golden wine-bowl (मद्यपात्रं), आर्षभाणि चर्माणि as shields covered in bull-hide (ऋषभचर्मपिनद्धानि फलकानि), and त्सरुः as a sword-hilt (खड्गमुष्टिः).

_____________

**3. The Celestial Proclamation (5.1.29–30)**

**Śivasahāya** notes that verses 29–30 form a single syntactic unit (श्लोकद्वयमेकान्वयि) reporting the words spoken by the sky-dwelling sages and Siddhas, while **Govindarāja** observes that **5.1.30** states the twin purpose (प्रयोजनकथनाय) of Hanumān’s crossing: serving both Rāma and the Vānaras.

_____________

**3. Gathering Threefold Power (5.1.36)**

**Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** explain भुजौ संहृत्य and शिरोधरां as drawing his arms into his shoulders (अंसप्रदेशे) and his neck into his throat (कण्ठदेशे). **Rāma Varmā** distinguishes the three powers Hanumān summons: तेजः is the capacity to overwhelm foes (पराभिभवसामर्थ्यम्), सत्त्वं is bodily strength (बलं शारीरम्), and वीर्यम् is inner vigor (आन्तरं उत्साहम्).

_____________

**3. Grammatical Notes on Hanumān’s Vow (5.1.42)**

Both **Śivasahāya** and **Govindarāja** explain the unshortened compound form राक्षसराजानम् by the non-obligatory character of the _samāsānta_ rule (समासान्तविधेरनित्यत्वान्न टच् / टच्प्रत्ययाभाव आर्षः), and gloss the epic future आनयिष्यामि as equivalent to आनेष्यामि ('I shall bring'). **Śivasahāya** adds that binding Rāvaṇa implies forcing him to reveal Sītā’s whereabouts (सीतावृत्तान्तं कथयिष्यत्येवेति सूचितम्).

_____________

**3. The Similes of Kinsmen and Soldiers (5.1.47–48)**

As the trees follow Hanumān briefly into the sky, Vālmīki compares them first to kinsmen accompanying a traveler (**5.1.47**) and then to soldiers marching behind a king (**5.1.48**). **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** note that सैन्य belongs to the _ardharcādi_ class (अर्धर्चादिः), while **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) cites the _Amarakośa_ (सेनायां समवेता ये सैन्यास्ते सैनिकाश्च ते) to confirm its meaning as 'soldiers'.

_____________

**3. Star-Strewn Sea and Lightning-Ringed Cloud (5.1.53–54)**

Carried further by their lightness (लघुत्वेनोपपन्नं), the multicolored blossoms scatter across the ocean like stars in the sky (**5.1.53**), while those clinging to Hanumān make him gleam like a rising cloud (मेघ इवोद्यन्) wreathed in lightning (**5.1.54**).

_____________

**3. The Twilight Sun Simile (5.1.60)**

All three commentators construe the instrumental ताम्रया नासिकया as 'endowed with a coppery nose' (नासिकया युक्तं), comparing Hanumān’s crimson face to the solar disc (सूर्यमण्डलं) tinged by evening twilight (संध्यया).

_____________

**3. The Girth-Bound Elephant (5.1.66)**

All three commentators gloss पतत्पतङ्गसंकाशः as resembling the coursing sun (गच्छत्सूर्यसंकाशः). **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** explain that Hanumān, encircled by his own tail (कपिवालेन), looks like a great elephant bound around the middle by a girth-rope (कक्ष्यया गजमध्यबन्धनरज्ज्वा), while **Govindarāja** (_Bhūṣaṇa_) adds that an elephant stretches out (दीर्घो भवति) while its girth is being tied.

_____________

**3. Twin Winds and Mountainous Billows (5.1.70–72)**

On **5.1.70**, **Rāma Varmā** and **Śivasahāya** distinguish कपिवातः (the gale raised by Hanumān’s body) from मेघवातः (the wind from the cloud-sphere he traverses), which together shake the sea as he bounds over Meru-like waves as if counting them (**5.1.72**).

_____________

**3. The Thirty-Yojana Shadow and Winged Mountain (5.1.76–78)**

Measuring ten _yojanas_ in width and thirty in length (**5.1.76**), Hanumān’s vast shadow trailing over the briny sea gleams like a dense bank of white clouds (श्वेताभ्रघनराजीव, **5.1.77**), while he himself soars through the supportless sky (निरालम्बे वायुमार्गे) like a winged mountain (**5.1.78**).

_____________

**3. Celestial Flower-Showers and Elemental Service (5.1.83–84)**

On **5.1.83**, **Rāma Varmā** construes तत्र locatively as 'upon that leaper' (तत्र प्लवगे). On **5.1.84**, all three commentators note that Sūrya refrains from scorching Hanumān and Vāyu blows gently to remove his fatigue (श्रमापनयनाय / श्रमनिवर्तनाय) so that Rāma’s mission—the search for Sītā (रामकार्यं सीतान्वेषणं)—may succeed.

_____________

**3. Offering a Mid-Ocean Resting Place (5.1.89–90)**

On **5.1.89**, **Rāma Varmā** notes that सगरेण विवर्धितः includes Sagara’s descendants (सगरवंश्यानामप्येतत्संबद्धः), and all three commentators gloss अवसादितुम् as 'to suffer exhaustion' (श्रमं गन्तुं नार्हति), resolving in **5.1.90** that Hanumān should rest mid-crossing before completing the remaining distance (मार्गशेषम्).

_____________

**3. Why the Ikṣvākus Are Supremely Worthy of Maināka’s Worship (5.1.96)**

**Govindarāja** gives two reasons why the Ikṣvākus are even more worshipful (पूज्यतमाः) to Maināka than to the Ocean: through his bond with the Ocean who shelters him (मत्संबन्धात्), or because Hanumān is the son of Vāyu, Maināka’s own benefactor (सहोपकारकवायुपुत्रसहायाः).

_____________

**3. Maināka’s Emergence Like the Sun from a Cloud (5.1.101–102)**

On **5.1.101**, all three commentators supply वचनम् ('speech') with the genitive लवणाम्भसः ('hearing the words of the salt sea'). On **5.1.102**, **Śivasahāya** (_Śiromaṇi_) highlights the simile (दृष्टान्तः) of golden Maināka cleaving the dark ocean water (सागरजलं भित्त्वा) just as the blazing-rayed sun (दीप्तरश्मिर्दिवाकरः) bursts through a dark rain-cloud (जलधरं मेघं भित्त्वा).
</details>
</details>

</details>

</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

प्रतिभाशालियाद आ हनुमन्तनु कॊब्बिद सिंहदन्तॆ विजृम्भिसिदनु. अवनु तिरुगाडुत्तिरुवाग अवन उब्बिद ऎदॆगॆ ऎदुरागि सिक्किद अनेक वृक्षगळु मुरिदु बिद्दुवु. वेगवागि अवनु होगुत्तिरुवाग पक्षिगळु भयगॊण्डु चदुरि हारिहोगुत्तिद्दवु. अनेक मृगगळु कालिन तुळितक्कॆ सिक्कि सत्तुहोदुवु.॥4॥
</details>

*nighnan* = killing; *subahuun* = many; *mR^igaaMshcha* = animals also.

by his breast and also killing many animals.

</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*5;6;7. saH* = that; *kapivaraH* = great monkey; *babhau*
= shone; *naagaH iva* = like an elephant; *hrade* = in a lake; *tishhThan* =
being located; *tasya girivarasya* = on that great mountain's; *tale* = foot;
*naagavaraayute* = which had best of elephants; *tatra* = there;
*samalaMkR^itam* = was decorated by; *svabhaavavihitaiH* = naturally formed;
*dhaatubhiH* = mineral rocks; *chitraiH* = of varied hues; *sitaasitaiH* =
(like) black and white; *niilalohitamaaJNjishhThapatravarNaiH* = blue; red; yellow; leaf
color (green); *abhiikshNam aavishhTam* = surrounded to a great extent by;
*yakshakinnaragandharvaiH* = Yakshas- Kinnaras-Gandharvas; *kaamaruupibhiH* = who
could assume desired form; *saparichchhadaiH* = together with their families; *cha deva
kalpaiH pannagaiH* = and godly serpents.

**अनुवादः**

That great monkey Hanuma shone like an elephant in a lake, standing on the foot of
that great mountain by the name of Mahendra, where many elephants of good breed resided, which was
beautifully decorated with many naturally formed mineral rocks of varied hue like black, white,
red, blue, yellow and green and which was surrounded by godly serpents and Yakshas, Kinnaras,
Gandharvas who were capable of assuming desired form, together with their families.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Standing there on the slope (_tale_) of that foremost of mountains, which abounded in lordly elephants (_nāgavarāyute_), the foremost of monkeys shone like a great elephant in a lake (_hrade nāga iva_).
</details>
</details>

</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हारिकॊण्डे समुद्रवन्नु दाटबेकॆन्दु दृढवागि निश्चयिसिद्द हनुमन्तनु श्रीरामन कार्यसिद्धिगागि, पर्वकालदल्लि समुद्रवु उक्केरुवन्तॆ मैयुब्बि बॆळॆयुत्तिरुवुदन्नु कपिनायकरॆल्लरू वीक्षिसिदरु.॥10॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*10. dR^ishhTaH* = being seen; *plavaN^gapravaraiH* = by best of
Vaanaras; *kR^ita nishchayaH* = with a strong resolve; *plavane* = to fly;
*vavR^idhe* = grew; *raama vR^iddhyarthaM* = for Rama's success; *samudraH
iva* = like the ocean; *parvasu* = on full moon days.

**अनुवादः**

With all the Vaanaras seeing, Hanuma decided to fly and like an ocean swelling on
full moon days, grew for Rama's success.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Beheld by the foremost of leapers (_plavagapravaraiḥ_) as one resolved upon the leap (_plavane kṛtaniścayaḥ_), he swelled for the sake of Rāma’s prosperity (_rāmavṛddhyartham_) like the ocean at the full-moon nodes (_parvasu_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

निष्प्रमाणशरीरः सल्ँ लिलङ्घयिषुरर्णवम् ।  
बाहुभ्यां पीडयामास चरणाभ्यां च पर्वतम् ॥ ११ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

निष्प्रमाणशरीरः सल्ँ लिलङ्घयिषुरर्णवम् ।  
बाहुभ्यां पीडयामास चरणाभ्यां च पर्वतम् ॥ ११ ॥
_________________
निष्प्रमाणशरीरःसन् लिलङ्घयिषुरर्णवम् ।  
बाहुभ्यां पीडयामास चरणाभ्यां च पर्वतम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

समुद्रको लाँघनेकी इच्छासे उन्होंने अपने शरीरको बेहद बढ़ा लिया और अपनी दोनों भुजाओं तथा चरणोंसे उस पर्वतको दबाया ॥ ११ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

सागरवन्नु लीलाजालवागि दाटलु मारुतियु अपरिमितवागि बॆळॆदु निन्तनु. हारलु उद्युक्तनाद अवनु महेन्द्र गिरियन्नु कैगळिन्दलू कालुगळिन्दलू बलवागि अदुमिदनु.॥11॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*11. lilaN^ghayishhuH* = Desiring to leap; *arNavam* = the
ocean; *nishhpramaaNa shariiraH* = with an immeasurable body; *piiDayaamaasa* =
pressed; *parvatam* = the mountain; *baahubhyaam* = with hands; *cha
charaNaabhyaam* = and feet.

**अनुवादः**

With a desire to cross the ocean, Hanuma grew his body immeasurably and pressed the
mountain with his feet and hands.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Possessing a body beyond measure (_niṣpramāṇaśarīraḥ_) and desiring to leap across (_lilaṅghayiṣuḥ_) the sea, he pressed down (_pīḍayām āsa_) the mountain with both his arms and his feet.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

स चचालाचलश्चाशु मुहूर्तं कपिपीडितः ।  
तरूणां पुष्पिताग्राणां सर्वं पुष्पमशातयत् ॥ १२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

स चचालाचलश्चाशु मुहूर्तं कपिपीडितः ।  
तरूणां पुष्पिताग्राणां सर्वं पुष्पमशातयत् ॥ १२ ॥
_________________
स चचालाचलश्चापि मुहूर्तं कपिपीडितः ।  
तरूणां पुष्पिताग्राणां सर्वं पुष्पमशातयत् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

कपिवर हनुमान् जी के द्वारा दबाये जानेपर तुरंत ही वह पर्वत काँप उठा और दो घड़ीतक डगमगाता रहा । उसके ऊपर जो वृक्ष उगे थे, उनकी डालियोंके अग्रभाग फूलोंसे लदे हुए थे; किंतु उस पर्वतके हिलनेसे उनके वे सारे फूल झड़ गये ॥ १२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

कपीश्वरनु आ पर्वतवन्नु अदुमिदाग स्वल्प हॊत्तिनवरॆगॆ अदु अलुगाडुत्तले इत्तु. मरगळ तुदियल्लि अरळिनिन्त पुष्पगळॆल्ल कॆळगुदुरिदवु.॥12॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*12. kapipiiDitaH* = tormented by Hanuma; *saH achalaH api* =
even that mountain; *chachaala* = shook; *muhuurtam* = for an instant;
*ashaatayat* = (and) caused to fall; *sarvam* = all; *pushhpam* = blossom;
*taruuNaam* = of trees; *pushhpitaagraaNaam* = with flowered ends.

**अनुवादः**

Thus tormented by Hanuma, that mountain shook for an instant and showered all
flowers of trees on it.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Pressed down by the monkey, that mountain (_acalaḥ_) shook (_cacāla_) swiftly for a moment (_muhūrtam_) and caused every blossom to fall (_aśātayat_) from the trees with flowering tops (_puṣpitāgrāṇām_).
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः - अवतारिका</summary>

<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Mantled in fallen blossoms, Mount Mahendra exudes water like a rutting elephant,extrudes veins of gold, antimony, and silver, casts out smoking boulders, and echoes with the cries of cave-creatures.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

तेन पादपमुक्तेन पुष्पौघेण सुगन्धिना ।  
सर्वतः संवृतः शैलो बभौ पुष्पमयो यथा ॥ १३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

तेन पादपमुक्तेन पुष्पौघेण सुगन्धिना ।  
सर्वतः संवृतः शैलो बभौ पुष्पमयो यथा ॥ १३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

तेन पादपमुक्तेन पुष्पौघेन सुगन्धिना ।
सर्वतः संवृतः शैलो बभौ पुष्पमयो यथा ॥५-१-१३
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

वृक्षोंसे झड़ी हुई उस सुगन्धित पुष्पराशिके द्वारा सब ओरसे आच्छादित हुआ वह पर्वत ऐसा जान पड़ता था, मानो वह फूलोंका ही बना हुआ हो ॥ १३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

कॆळक्कुदुरिद सुगन्ध युक्तवाद पुष्पराशियिन्द आ पर्वतवु तुम्बिहोयितु. तत्कालदल्लि अदु हूविनदे पर्वतवो ऎम्बन्तॆ काणुत्तित्तु.॥13॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*13. saMvR^itaH* = covered; *sarvataH* = all through;
*sugandhinaa pushhpaughena* = by groups of fragrant flowers; *tena paadapamuktena* =
left by those trees; *shailaH* = (that) mountain; *babhau* = shone; *yathaa* =
like; *pushhpamayo* = being made of flowers.

**अनुवादः**

Covered all through by those fragrant flowers which fell down from the trees, that
mountain shone like a mountain made of flowers.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Covered on all sides (_sarvataḥ saṃvṛtaḥ_) by that fragrant mass of flowers (_puṣpaugheṇa_) shed from the trees (_pādapamuktena_), the mountain shone as though made entirely of flowers (_puṣpamayo yathā_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

तेन चोत्तमवीर्येण पीड्यमानः स पर्वतः ।  
सलिलं सम्प्रसुस्राव मदमत्त इव द्विपः ॥ १४ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

तेन चोत्तमवीर्येण पीड्यमानः स पर्वतः ।  
सलिलं सम्प्रसुस्राव मदमत्त इव द्विपः ॥ १४ ॥
_________________
तेन चोत्तमवीर्येण पीड्यमानः स पर्वतः ।  
सलिलं सम्प्रसुस्राव मदं मत्त इव द्विपः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

महापराक्रमी हनुमान् जी के द्वारा दबाया जाता हुआ महेन्द्रपर्वत जलके स्रोत बहाने लगा, मानो कोई मदमत्त गजराज अपने कुम्भस्थलसे मदकी धारा बहा रहा हो ॥ १४ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

महापराक्रमियाद हनुमन्तनिन्द अदुमल्पट्ट आ पर्वतवु, मदिसिद आनॆयु तन्न गण्डस्थलदिन्द मदोदकवन्नु सुरिसुवन्तॆ, ऎल्ल कडॆगळिन्दलू नीरन्नु हॊरचिम्मिसितु.॥14॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*14. saH parvataH* = that mountain; *saMprasusraava* = shed;
*salilam* = water; *madam iva* = like rut juice; *mathaH dvipaH* = (from) an
elephant in rut; *piiDyamaanaH* = being pressed; *tena uttama viiryeNa* = by that
powerful Hanuma.

**अनुवादः**

That mountain shed water like an elephant in rut emitting rut juice, after being
pressed by the mighty Hanuma.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

And pressed down (_pīḍyamānaḥ_) by that hero of supreme valor (_uttamavīryeṇa_), that mountain exuded water (_salilaṃ samprasusrāva_) like a rutting elephant (_madamatta iva dvipaḥ_) [exuding ichor].
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

पीड्यमानस्तु बलिना महेन्द्रस्तेन पर्वतः ।  
रीतीर्निर्वर्तयामास काञ्चनाञ्जनराजतीः ॥ १५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

पीड्यमानस्तु बलिना महेन्द्रस्तेन पर्वतः ।  
रीतीर्निर्वर्तयामास काञ्चनाञ्जनराजतीः ॥ १५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

बलवान् पवनकुमारके भारसे दबा हुआ महेन्द्रगिरि सुनहरे, रुपहले और काले रंगके जलस्रोत प्रवाहित करने लगा ॥ १५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

महा बलिष्ठनाद मारुतिय कै-कालुगळिन्द अदुमल्टट्ट महेन्द्रपर्वतवु तन्नल्लि अडगिद्द बङ्गार, बॆळ्ळि, कब्बिण मुन्ताद धातुगळन्नु हॊरहाकितु.॥15॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*15. piiDyamaanaH* = tormented; *tena balinaa* = by that mighty
Hanuma; *riitiiH* = lines; *kaaJNchanaaJNjana raajatiiH* = gold; silver and black
kind; *nirvartayaamaasa* = were made to form; *mahendraH parvataH* = (on) the
mountain of Mahendra.

**अनुवादः**

Pressed by that mighty Hanuma, streaks of gold silver and antimony color appeared on
the Mahendra mountain.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Pressed down by that mighty one (_balinā_), Mount Mahendra brought forth (_nirvartayām āsa_) streaks (_rītīḥ_) of gold, dark antimony, and silver (_kāñcanāñjanarājatīḥ_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

मुमोच च शिलाः शैलो विशालाः समनःशिलाः ।  
मध्यमेनार्चिषा जुष्टो धूमराजीरिवानलः ॥ १६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

मुमोच च शिलाः शैलो विशालाः समनःशिलाः ।  
मध्यमेनार्चिषा जुष्टो धूमराजीरिवानलः ॥ १६ ॥
_________________
मुमोच च शिलाः शैलो विशालाः सुमनःशिलाः ।  
मध्यमेनार्चिषा जुष्टो धूमराजीरिवानलः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

इतना ही नहीं, जैसे मध्यम ज्वालासे युक्त अग्नि लगातार धुआँ छोड़ रही हो, उसी प्रकार वह पर्वत मैनसिलसहित बड़ी-बड़ी शिलाएँ गिराने लगा ॥ १६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

महा अग्निय सप्तार्चिगळल्लि ‘सुलोहित’* ऎम्बुदु मध्य मार्चियु अदर ज्वालॆगळिन्द हॊगॆयु हॊर सूसुवन्तॆ आञ्जनेयनिन्द अदुमल्पट्ट आ महेन्द्र गिरियिन्द मणि शिलॆगळिन्द युक्तवाद दॊड्ड-दॊड्ड बण्डॆगळु सिडिदु पुडि-पुडियागि बिद्दवु.**॥16॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*16. shailaH* = mountain; *mumocha cha* = also emitted;
*vishaalaaH* = vast; *shilaaH* = boulders; *samanaH shilaaH* = with rocks of
Sulfur; *dhuumaraajiiriva* = like columns of smoke; *analaH* = (from a) fire;
*jushhTaH* = with; *madhyamena archishhaa* = with a medium flame.

**अनुवादः**

Huge Rocks of Sulfur were also shot forth from that mountain, just as a fire burning
with a medium flame would emit columns of smoke.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

And the mountain cast forth huge boulders (_śilāḥ viśālāḥ_) mixed with red arsenic (_samanaḥśilāḥ_), just as a fire endowed with its middle flame (_madhyamenārciṣā juṣṭaḥ_) sends forth streaks of smoke (_dhūmarājīr iva_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

हरिणा पीड्यमानेन पीड्यमानानि सर्वतः ।  
गुहाविष्टानि सत्त्वानि विनेदुर्विकृतैः स्वरैः ॥ १७ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

हरिणा पीड्यमानेन पीड्यमानानि सर्वतः ।  
गुहाविष्टानि सत्त्वानि विनेदुर्विकृतैः स्वरैः ॥ १७ ॥
_________________
गिरिणा पीड्यमानेन पीड्यमानानि सर्वतः ।  
गुहाविष्टानि भूतानि विनेदुर्विकृतैः स्वरैः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

गिरिणा पीड्यमानेन पीड्यमानानि सर्वशः ।
गुहाविष्टानि भूतानि विनेदुर्विकृतैः स्वरैः ॥५-१-१७
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

हनुमान् जी के उस पर्वत-पीडनसे पीड़ित होकर वहाँके समस्त जीव गुफाओंमें घुसे हुए बुरी तरहसे चिल्लाने लगे ॥ १७ ॥
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Pressed down on every side by the mountain that was itself being pressed by the monkey (_hariṇā pīḍyamānena_), the creatures (_sattvāni_) dwelling in its caves cried out with distorted voices (_vikṛtaiḥ svaraiḥ_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

स महान् सत्त्वसन्नादः शैलपीडानिमित्तजः ।  
पृथिवीं पूरयामास दिशश्चोपवनानि च ॥ १८ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

स महान् सत्त्वसन्नादः शैलपीडानिमित्तजः ।  
पृथिवीं पूरयामास दिशश्चोपवनानि च ॥ १८ ॥
_________________
स महासत्त्वसन्नादः शैलपीडानिमित्तजः ।  
पृथिवीं पूरयामास दिशश्चोपवनानि च ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

इस प्रकार पर्वतको दबानेके कारण उत्पन्न हुआ वह जीव-जन्तुओंका महान् कोलाहल पृथ्वी, उपवन और सम्पूर्ण दिशाओंमें भर गया ॥ १८ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हनुमन्तनिन्द अदुमल्पट्ट पर्वतवु कम्पिसिदाग, पर्वतद गुहॆगळल्लिद्द ऎल्ल प्राणिगळु तॊन्दरॆगॊळगागि ऎल्ल कडॆगळिन्दलू विकार स्वरगळिन्द कूगिकॊण्डवु. पर्वतद कम्पनदिन्द गुहॆगळल्लि वास माडुत्तिद्द ऎल्ल प्राणिगळु भयदिन्द आर्तनादगळन्नु माडिदवु आ आक्रन्दन ध्वनियु भूमण्डलवन्नु, उपवनगळन्नु, हत्तु दिक्कुगळन्नु व्यापिसितु.॥17-18॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*17. piiDyamaanaani* = being pressed; *sarvashaH* = on all
sides; *giriNaa* = by the mountain; *piiDyamaanena* = which was being tormented (by
Hanuma); *bhuutaani* = creatures; *guhaavishhTaani* = living in caves;
*vineduH* = shrieked; *vikR^itaiH* = in horrifying; *svaraiH* = tones.

**अनुवादः**

Being pressed on all sides by the mountain which itself was being pressed by Hanuma,
creatures living in the caves of that mountain shrieked in horrifying tones.
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*18. saH* = that; *mahaasattvasaMnaadaH* = loud noise from
creatures; *shailapiiDaanimittajaH* = formed because of the stress to the mountain;
*puurayaamaasa* = filled; *pR^ithiviim* = the earth; *cha dishaH* = all the
directions; *cha upavanaani* = and forests (near that mountain).

**अनुवादः**

That loud noise from creatures which emanated because of stress caused to the
mountain, filled the earth and all four directions and forests near that mountain.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

That great uproar of creatures (_sattvasannādaḥ_), born from the cause of the mountain’s crushing (_śailapīḍānimittajaḥ_), filled the earth, the quarters of space, and the groves (_upavanāni ca_).
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः - अवतारिका</summary>

<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Enraged serpents bite the rocks until they blaze and shatter beyond the power of healing herbs, while terrified ascetics and Vidyādharas flee into the sky, abandoning their banqueting gear and weapons.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

शिरोभिः पृथुभिर्नागा व्यक्तस्वस्तिकलक्षणैः ।  
वमन्तः पावकं घोरं ददंशुर्दशनैः शिलाः ॥ १९ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

शिरोभिः पृथुभिर्नागा व्यक्तस्वस्तिकलक्षणैः ।  
वमन्तः पावकं घोरं ददंशुर्दशनैः शिलाः ॥ १९ ॥
_________________
शिरोभिः पृथुभिः सर्पा व्यक्तस्वस्तिकलक्षणैः ।  
वमन्तः पावकं घोरं ददंशुर्दशनैः शिलाः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

जिनमें स्वस्तिक* चिह्न स्पष्ट दिखायी दे रहे थे, उन स्थूल फणोंसे विषकी भयानक आग उगलते हुए बड़े-बड़े सर्प उस पर्वतकी शिलाओंको अपने दाँतोंसे डँसने लगे ॥ १९ ॥

\\* साँपके फनोंमें दिखायी देनेवाली नील रेखाको ‘स्वस्तिक’ कहते हैं ।
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

स्पष्टवागि काणुव स्वस्तिक लक्षणगळिन्द कूडिकॊण्डिद्द दॊड्ड-दॊड्ड हॆडॆगळन्नु हॊन्दिद्द भारी सर्पगळु पर्वतवु नडुगुविकॆयिन्द खतिगॊण्डु मुखदिन्द घोरवाद विषाग्नियन्नु कारुत्ता विषद हल्लुगळिन्द बण्डॆगळन्ने कच्चुत्तिद्दवु.॥19॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*19. sarpaaH* = Snakes; *vyakta svastika lakshaNaiH* = with
clear swastika signs (on the hoods); *vamantaH* = spewed out; *ghoram paavakam* =
frightening flame; *pR^ithubhiH shirobhiH* = with (their) great heads; *dadaMshuH* =
(and) bit; *shilaaH* = rocks; *dashanaiH* = with (their) teeth.

**अनुवादः**

Great snakes with clear swastika signs on their hoods, spewed out frightening flames
with their great heads and bit the rocks with their teeth.
</details>



<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

With their broad hoods (_śirobhiḥ pṛthubhiḥ_) bearing distinct svastika markings (_vyaktasvastikalakṣaṇaiḥ_), Nāgas vomiting dreadful fire (_pāvakaṃ ghoram_) bit the rocks with their fangs (_daśanaiḥ_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

तास्तदा सविषैर्दष्टाः कुपितैस्तैर्महाशिलाः ।  
जज्वलुः पावकोद्दीप्ता बिभिदुश्च सहस्रधा ॥ २० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

तास्तदा सविषैर्दष्टाः कुपितैस्तैर्महाशिलाः ।  
जज्वलुः पावकोद्दीप्ता बिभिदुश्च सहस्रधा ॥ २० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

तास्तदा सविषैर्दष्टाः कुपितैस्तैर्महाशिलाः।
जज्ज्वलुः पावकोद्दीप्ता बिभिदुश्च सहस्रधा ॥५-१-२०
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

क्रोधसे भरे हुए उन विषैले साँपोंके काटनेपर वे बड़ी-बड़ी शिलाएँ इस प्रकार जल उठीं, मानो उनमें आग लग गयी हो । उस समय उन सबके सहस्रों टुकड़े हो गये ॥ २० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

कोपगॊण्ड आ महाविषसर्पगळिन्द कच्चल्पट्ट महेन्द्रपर्वतद दॊड्ड-दॊड्ड बण्डॆगळु अग्नियिन्द उद्दीपनगॊण्डु प्रज्वलिसुत्ता क्षणमात्रदल्लि साविरारु चूरु चुरुगळागि ऒडॆदुहोदवु.॥20॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*20. tadaa* = then; *taaH mahaashilaaH* = those great rocks;
*dashhTaaH* = bit; *taiH* = by those (snakes); *kupitaiH* = with anger;
*savishhaiH* = (and) with venom; *jajvaluH* = burned; *paavakoddiiptaaH* =
encouraged by the flame; *bibhidushcha* = and split; *sahasrathaa* = into thousand
pieces.

**अनुवादः**

Then those great rocks bit by those snakes with anger and venom, burned due to the
flame and split into thousand pieces.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Bitten then by those enraged, venomous serpents (_saviṣaiḥ_), those great boulders blazed up (_jajvaluḥ_), kindled with fire (_pāvakoddīptāḥ_), and split apart a thousandfold (_sahasradhā_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

यानि त्वौषधजालानि तस्मिञ्जातानि पर्वते ।  
विषघ्नान्यपि नागानां न शेकुः शमितुं विषम् ॥ २१ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

यानि त्वौषधजालानि तस्मिञ्जातानि पर्वते ।  
विषघ्नान्यपि नागानां न शेकुः शमितुं विषम् ॥ २१ ॥
_________________
यानि औषधजालानि तस्मिन्जातानि पर्वते ।  
विषघ्नान्यपि नागानां न शेकुः शमितुं विषम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

यानि चौषधजालानि तस्मिन् जातानि पर्वते ।
विषघ्नान्यपि नागानां न शेकुः शमितुं विषम्॥ ५-१-२१
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उस पर्वतपर जो बहुत-सी ओषधियाँ उगी हुई थीं, वे विषको नष्ट करनेवाली होनेपर भी उन नागोंके विषको शान्त न कर सकीं ॥ २१ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

आ पर्वतदल्ले हुट्टि बॆळॆदिद्द विषवन्नु होगलाडिसलु समर्थवागिद्द औषधिलतॆगळ समूहगळु कूड खतिगॊण्ड सर्पगळ विषवन्नु उपशमनगॊळिसलु असमर्थवादुवु.॥21॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*21. yaani aushhadhajaalaani* = whatever medicinal herbs;
*jaataani* = born; *tasmin parvate* = on that mountain; *vishhaghnaanyapi* =
although destroyers of poison; *na shekuH* = (were) not capable; *shamitum* = of
neutralizing; *vishham* = poison; *naagaanaam* = of the snakes.

**अनुवादः**

Medicinal herbs from that mountain, although capable of destroying ordinary poisons,
could not neutralize the poison from those snakes.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

And whatever clusters of medicinal herbs (_auṣadhajālāni_) grew upon that mountain, though potent to destroy venom (_viṣaghnāny api_), could not (_na śekuḥ_) quell the venom of those Nāgas.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

भिद्यतेऽयं गिरिर्भूतैरिति मत्वा तपस्विनः ।  
त्रस्ता विद्याधरास्तस्मादुत्पेतुः स्त्रीगणैः सह ॥ २२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

भिद्यतेऽयं गिरिर्भूतैरिति मत्वा तपस्विनः ।  
त्रस्ता विद्याधरास्तस्मादुत्पेतुः स्त्रीगणैः सह ॥ २२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उस समय वहाँ रहनेवाले तपस्वी और विद्याधरोंने समझा कि इस पर्वतको भूतलोग तोड़ रहे हैं, इससे भयभीत होकर वे अपनी स्त्रियोंके साथ वहाँसे ऊपर उठकर अन्तरिक्षमें चले गये ॥ २२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

यावुदो महाप्राणिगळिन्द ई महेन्द्र पर्वतवु भेदिसल्पडुत्तिदॆ ऎन्दु भाविसि, अल्लि आश्रयवन्नु पडॆदिद्द तपस्विगळु भयगॊण्डु अल्लिन्द धाविसिदरु. विद्याधररू भयदिन्द तम्म स्त्रीयरॊडनॆ अन्तरिक्षद कडॆगॆ हारिदरु.॥22॥
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Thinking, &#x27;This mountain is being cleft by elemental powers (_bhūtaiḥ_)!&#x27;, ascetics (_tapasvinaḥ_) and terrified Vidyādharas sprang up (_utpetuḥ_) from it together with their troops of women (_strīgaṇaiḥ saha_),
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः - अवतारिका</summary>

<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Garlanded Vidyādharas and their jewel-decked consorts hover in the sky, hearing the celestial sages, Cāraṇas, and Siddhas proclaim Hanumān’s resolve to leap across Varuṇa’s ocean for Rāma’s sake.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

पानभूमिगतं हित्वा हैममासवभाजनम् ।  
पात्राणि च महार्हाणि करकांश्च हिरण्मयान् ॥ २३ ॥  
लेह्यानुच्चावचान् भक्ष्यान् मांसानि विविधानि च ।  
आर्षभाणि च चर्माणि खड्गांश्च कनकत्सरून् ॥ २४ ॥  
कृतकण्ठगुणाः क्षीबा रक्तमाल्यानुलेपनाः ।  
रक्ताक्षाः पुष्कराक्षाश्च गगनं प्रतिपेदिरे ॥ २५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

पानभूमिगतं हित्वा हैममासवभाजनम् ।  
पात्राणि च महार्हाणि करकांश्च हिरण्मयान् ॥ २३ ॥  
लेह्यानुच्चावचान् भक्ष्यान् मांसानि विविधानि च ।  
आर्षभाणि च चर्माणि खड्गांश्च कनकत्सरून् ॥ २४ ॥  
कृतकण्ठगुणाः क्षीबा रक्तमाल्यानुलेपनाः ।  
रक्ताक्षाः पुष्कराक्षाश्च गगनं प्रतिपेदिरे ॥ २५ ॥
_________________
पानभूमिगतं हित्वा हैममासवभाजनम् ।  
पात्राणि च महार्हाणि कर्कांश्च हिरण्मयान् ॥
लेह्यानुच्चावचान् भक्ष्यान् मांसानि विविधानि च ।  
आर्षभाणि च चर्माणि खड्गांश्च कनकत्सरून् ॥
कृतकण्ठगुणाः क्षीबा रक्तमाल्यानुलेपनाः ।  
रक्ताक्षाः पुष्कराक्षाश्च गगनं प्रतिपेदिरे ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

मधुपानके स्थानमें रखे हुए सुवर्णमय आसवपात्र, बहुमूल्य बर्तन, सोनेके कलश, भाँति-भाँतिके भक्ष्य पदार्थ, चटनी, नाना प्रकारके फलोंके गूदे, बैलोंकी खालकी बनी हुई ढालें और सुवर्णजटित मूठवाली तलवारें छोड़कर कण्ठमें माला धारण किये, लाल रंगके फूल और अनुलेपन (चन्दन) लगाये, प्रफुल्ल कमलके सदृश सुन्दर एवं लाल नेत्रवाले वे मतवाले विद्याधरगण भयभीत-से होकर आकाशमें चले गये ॥ २३—२५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

अवरु कण्ठाभरणगळन्नु, कॆम्पु हूविन मालॆगळन्नु धरिसि, गन्धवन्नु पूसिकॊण्डिद्दरु. कॆङ्गण्णरू, कमलाक्षरू आगिद्दु, पान भूमियल्लि मद्यासवगळन्नु सेविसुत्ता आनन्द तुन्दिलरागिद्द विद्याधररु भयदिन्द आकाशक्कॆ नॆगॆदरु.॥25॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

22; 23; *24. tapasvinaH* = ascetics; *mattvaa* = thought;
*iti* = that; *ayam* = this giriH = mountain; *bhidyate* = is being shattered;
*bhuutaiH* = by bhutas; *utpetuH* = (and) flew away; *vidhyaadharaaH* =
Vidhyaadharaas; *trastaaH* = who became afraid; *hitvaa* = left; *haimam* =
golden; *aasavabhaajanam* = jugs of wine; *paanabhuumigatam* = in the liquor house;
*mahaarhaaNi* = golden hued; *karakaaMshcha* = vases; *uchchaavachaan* = lot
of; *lehyaan* = sauces to be licked; *bhakshyaan* = eatables; *vividhaani* =
various; *maaMsaani* = meats; *charmaaNi* = skins; *aarshhabhaaNi* = of oxen;
*khaDgaaMshcha* = and swords; *kanakatsaruun* = with gold hilts; *utpetuH* =
(and) flew; *strii ganaiH saha* = along with their women folk.

**अनुवादः**

Ascetics residing on that mountain flew away from there thinking that some demons
were destroying it. Vidhyadharas who lived there, became afraid and flew away with their women
folk, leaving behind them golden jugs of wine in the liquor house, gold vases, a varieties of
sauces that can be licked, eatables, various meats, skins of oxen and swords with golden hilts.
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

अवरु नानाविधवाद लेह्यगळन्नु, बगॆबगॆय भक्ष्यगळन्नु, चॆन्नागि मागिद विविध मधुर रस फलगळन्नु, ऎत्तिन चर्मदिन्द माडिद गुराणिगळन्नु, चिन्नद हिडिगळिन्द कूडिद कत्तिगळन्नु परित्यजिसि आकाशवन्नु सेरिदरु.॥24॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

आ विद्याधररु पानभूमियल्लिद्द मधुर पानीयगळन्नु, बङ्गारद पात्रॆगळन्नु, अमूल्यवाद भोजन पात्रॆगळन्नु, कनक कलशगळन्नु भयदिन्द अल्लॆ बिट्टु होदरु.॥23॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*25. kshiibaaH* = the intoxicated (Vidhyadharas); *kR^ita kanTha
guNaaH* = with garlands around neck; *rakta maalyaanulepanaaH* = with red flower
garlands and smeared with sandal paste; *raktaakshaaH* = with reddened eyes;
*pushhparaakshaashcha* = and with lotus like eyes; *pratipedire* = obtained;
*gaganam* = the sky.

**अनुवादः**

The intoxicated Vidyadharas with garlands around their neck decked with red flower
garlands and smeared with sandal paste, with reddened eyes, and with lotus shaped eyes, obtained
the sky.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

**23:** Abandoning (_hitvā_) in the drinking-grounds (_pānabhūmigatam_) their golden wine-vessels (_haimam āsavabhājanam_), costly goblets (_pātrāṇi ca mahārhāṇi_), and golden pitchers (_karakāṃś ca hiraṇmayān_),

**24:** High and low delicacies to be licked (_lehyān uccāvacān_), eatables (_bhakṣyān_), meats of many kinds (_māṃsāni vividhāni ca_), bull-hide shields (_ārṣabhāṇi ca carmāṇi_), and golden-hilted swords (_khaḍgāṃś ca kanakatsarūn_).

**25:** Wearing neck-garlands (_kṛtakaṇṭhaguṇāḥ_), flushed with wine (_kṣībāḥ_), adorned with red garlands and unguents (_raktamālyānulepanāḥ_), red-eyed and lotus-eyed (_puṣkarākṣāḥ_), they betook themselves to the sky (_gaganaṃ pratipedire_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

हारनूपुरकेयूरपारिहार्यधराः स्त्रियः ।  
विस्मिताः सस्मितास्तस्थुराकाशे रमणौः सह ॥ २६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

हारनूपुरकेयूरपारिहार्यधराः स्त्रियः ।  
विस्मिताः सस्मितास्तस्थुराकाशे रमणौः सह ॥ २६ ॥
_________________
हारनूपुरकेयूरपारिहार्यधराः स्त्रीयः ।  
विस्मिताः सस्मितास्तस्थुराकाशे रमणैः सह ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

हारनूपुरकेयूरपारिहार्यधराः स्त्रियः ।
विस्मिताः सस्मितास्तस्थुराकाशे रमणैः सह ॥५-१-२६
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उनकी स्त्रियाँ गलेमें हार, पैरोंमें नूपुर,भुजाओंमें बाजूबंद और कलाइयोंमें कंगन धारण किये आकाशमें अपने पतियोंके साथ मन्द-मन्द मुसकराती हुई चकित-सी खड़ी हो गयीं ॥ २६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हारगळन्नु, कालन्दुगॆगळन्नु, तोळबन्दिगॆगळन्नु, बळॆगळन्नु धरिसिद्द विद्याधर स्त्रीयरु हनुमन्तन बृहदाकारवन्नु, पर्वतवन्नु अल्लाडिसुत्तिद्द अवन हॆज्जॆगळ सङ्घटनॆयन्नु नोडुत्ता, सम्भ्रमाश्चर्यगळिन्द, मन्दहासदिन्द तम्म प्रियकररॊडगूडि अन्तरिक्षदल्लि निन्तिद्दरु.॥26॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*26. striiyaH* = Women; *haara nuupura keyuura paarihaarya
dharaaH* = wearing necklaces; anklets; armlets and bangles; *tastuH* = stood;
*aakaashe* = in the sky; *vishmitaaH* = with surprise; *sasmitaaH* = (and)
with smiles; *ramaNaiH saH* = along with their loved ones.

**अनुवादः**

Vidyadhara women wearing necklaces, anklets, armlets and bangles stood in the sky
with surprise and with smiles along with their loved ones.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Women wearing pearl necklaces, anklets, armlets, and bracelets (_hāranūpurakeyūrapārihāryadharāḥ_), amazed (_vismitāḥ_) and smiling (_sasmitāḥ_), stood in the sky alongside their lovers (_ramaṇaiḥ saha_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

दर्शयन्तो महाविद्यां विद्याधरमहर्षयः ।  
सहितास्तस्थुराकाशे वीक्षाञ्चक्रुश्च पर्वतम् ॥ २७ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

दर्शयन्तो महाविद्यां विद्याधरमहर्षयः ।  
सहितास्तस्थुराकाशे वीक्षाञ्चक्रुश्च पर्वतम् ॥ २७ ॥
_________________
दर्शयन्तो महाविद्यां विद्याधरमहर्षयः ।  
सप्रियास्तस्थुराकाशे वीक्षाञ्चक्रुश्च पर्वतम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

विद्याधर और महर्षि अपनी महाविद्या (आकाशमें निराधार खड़े होनेकी शक्ति)-का परिचय देते हुए अन्तरिक्षमें एक साथ खड़े हो गये और उस पर्वतकी ओर देखने लगे ॥ २७ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हीगॆ विद्याधररू, महर्षिगळू निरालम्बरागि आकाशदल्लि निन्तुकॊण्डु, तम्म वायुस्तम्भनवॆम्ब महाविद्यॆयन्नु प्रदर्शिसुत्ता जॊतॆगूडि निन्तु, तावु परित्यजिसिद महेन्द्र पर्वतवन्नु कूतूहलदिन्द विक्षिसुत्तिद्दरु.॥27॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*27. vidyaadhara maharshhayaH* = Vidyadharas and great sages;
*tastuH* = stood; *aakaashe* = in the sky; *sahitaaH* = in a group;
*darshayantaH* = showing; *mahaavidyaam* = great prowess;
*viikshaaJNchakrushcha* = and viewed; *parvatam* = the mountain.

**अनुवादः**

Vidyadharas and great sages stood in the sky in a group, showing their great prowess
and viewed the mountain.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Displaying their great mystic lore (_mahāvidyām_), the Vidyādharas and great sages (_vidyādharamaharṣayaḥ_) stood together in the sky and gazed down upon the mountain (_vīkṣāṃ cakruś ca parvatam_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

शुश्रुवुश्च तदा शब्दमृषीणां भावितात्मनाम् ।  
चारणानां च सिद्धानां स्थितानां विमलेऽम्बरे ॥ २८ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

शुश्रुवुश्च तदा शब्दमृषीणां भावितात्मनाम् ।  
चारणानां च सिद्धानां स्थितानां विमलेऽम्बरे ॥ २८ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उन्होंने उस समय निर्मल आकाशमें खड़े हुए भावितात्मा (पवित्र अन्तःकरणवाले) महर्षियों, चारणों और सिद्धोंकी ये बातें सुनीं— ॥ २८ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

अवरु आग तम्मन्तॆये आकाशदल्लि निन्तिद्द पवित्रान्तःकरणराद महर्षिगळू, सिद्ध-चारणरू आडुत्तिद्द मातुगळन्नु केळिदरु.॥28॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*28. tadaa* = then; *shushruvuH* = (they) heard;
*shabdam* = words; *charaNaanaam* = of Charanas; *siddhaanaam* = Siddhas;
*cha R^ishhiNaam* = and Rishis; *bhaavitaatmaanaam* = with pure hearts;
*sthitaanaam* = located; *vimale ambare* = in (that) clear sky.

**अनुवादः**

Then they heard words of Charanas, Siddhas and pure hearted Rishis who were located
in that clear sky.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

And then they heard the utterance (_śabdam_) of sages of purified souls (_bhāvitātmanām_), Cāraṇas, and Siddhas stationed in the cloudless sky (_vimale &#x27;mbare_):
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

एष पर्वतसङ्काशो हनुमान् मारुतात्मजः ।  
तितीर्षति महावेगः समुद्रं वरुणालयम् ॥ २९ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

एष पर्वतसङ्काशो हनुमान् मारुतात्मजः ।  
तितीर्षति महावेगः समुद्रं वरुणालयम् ॥ २९ ॥
_________________
एष पर्वतसङ्काशो हनूमान् मारुतात्मजः ।  
तितीर्षति महावेगः सागरं मकरालयम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

एष पर्वतसंकाशो हनूमान् मारुतात्मजः ।
तितीर्षति महावेगः समुद्रं मकरालयम् ॥५-१-२९
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

‘अहा! ये पर्वतके समान विशालकाय महान् वेगशाली पवनपुत्र हनुमान् जी वरुणालय समुद्रको पार करना चाहते हैं ॥ २९ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

‘‘पर्वताकारनाद वायुपुत्रनू, महावेग शालियू आद हनुमन्तनु वरुणन निवासस्थानवाद ई समुद्रवन्नु दाटलु मनस्सु माडिद्दानॆ.॥29॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*29. eshhaH* = this; *hanumaan* = Hanuma;
*parvatasaMkaashaH* = who is equal to a mountain; *maarutaatmajaH* = who is the son
of Vayu; *mahaavegaH* = with great speed; *titiirshhati* = wants to cross;
*samudram* = the ocean; *makaraalayam* = which is abode to crocodiles.

**अनुवादः**

"This Hanuma, who is equal to a mountain, who is the son of Vayu, and who has great
speed, wants to cross the ocean which is abode to crocodiles."
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

“Here is mountain-like Hanumān, son of Māruta (_mārutātmajaḥ_); possessed of great speed (_mahāvegaḥ_), he desires to cross (_titīrṣati_) the ocean, the abode of Varuṇa (_varuṇālayam_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

रामार्थं वानरार्थं च चिकीर्षन् कर्म दुष्करम् ।  
समुद्रस्य परं पारं दुष्प्रापं प्राप्तुमिच्छति ॥ ३० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

रामार्थं वानरार्थं च चिकीर्षन् कर्म दुष्करम् ।  
समुद्रस्य परं पारं दुष्प्रापं प्राप्तुमिच्छति ॥ ३० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

‘श्रीरामचन्द्रजी और वानरोंके कार्यकी सिद्धिके लिये दुष्कर कर्म करनेकी इच्छा रखनेवाले ये पवनकुमार समुद्रके दूसरे तटपर पहुँचना चाहते हैं, जहाँ जाना अत्यन्त कठिन है’ ॥ ३० ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

श्रीरामन कार्यसिद्धिगागियू, वानरराजनाद सुग्रीवन अप्पणॆमेरॆगॆ दुष्करवाद कार्यवन्नु माडलु बयसिरुवनु. इवनु अतिकष्टसाध्यवाद ई समुद्रद आचॆय दडवन्नु सेरलु इच्छिसिद्दानॆ.’’॥30॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*30. ichchhati* = (He) wants; *praaptum* = to obtain;
*samudrasya param paaram* = the other side of ocean; *dushhpraapam* = which is hard
to obtain; *chikiirshhan* = (and) has decided to perform; *dushhkaram* = (this)
impossible; *karma* = task; *raamaartham* = for Rama; *vaanaraaratham* = and
for Vanaras.

**अनुवादः**

"Hanuma has decided to perform an impossible task for the sake of Rama and Vanaras
and wants to obtain the other side of ocean which is hard to obtain."
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Desiring to perform (_cikīrṣan_) a feat hard to accomplish for the sake of Rāma and the Vānaras (_rāmārthaṃ vānarārthaṃ ca_), he wishes to reach the further shore (_paraṃ pāram_) of the ocean, which is hard to attain.”
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः - अवतारिका</summary>

<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Beholding Hanumān from the sky, the Vidyādharas watch him shake his pelt, roar like a thundercloud, lash his serpent-like tail, crouch on his haunches, and summon his inner power.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

इति विद्याधरा वाचः श्रुत्वा तेषां तपस्विनाम् ।  
तमप्रमेयं ददृशुः पर्वते वानरर्षभम् ॥ ३१ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

इति विद्याधरा वाचः श्रुत्वा तेषां तपस्विनाम् ।  
तमप्रमेयं ददृशुः पर्वते वानरर्षभम् ॥ ३१ ॥
_________________
इति विद्याधराः श्रुत्वा वचस्तेषां तपस्विनाम् ।  
तमप्रमेयं ददृशुः पर्वते वानरर्षभम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

इति विद्याधराः श्रुत्वा वचस्तेषां महात्मनाम् ।
तमप्रमेयं ददृशुः पर्वते वानरर्षभम् ॥५-१-३१
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

इस प्रकार विद्याधरोंने उन तपस्वी महात्माओंकी कही हुई ये बातें सुनकर पर्वतके ऊपर अतुलितबलशाली वानरशिरोमणि हनुमान् जी को देखा ॥ ३१ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हीगॆ निर्मलवाद आकाशदल्लि निन्तिद्द सिद्ध-चारणरू मत्तु तपस्विगळू हेळुत्तिद्द मातुगळन्नु केळि विद्याधररु कुतूहलाविष्टरागि महेन्द्र पर्वतदल्लिद्द, अप्रमेयनाद, वानरश्रेष्ठनाद हनुमन्तनन्नु नोडुत्तिद्दरु.॥31॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*31. vidyadharaaH* = Vidyadharas; *shrutvaa* = listened;
*vachaH* = words; *teshhaam mahaatmaanaam* = of those great people; *iti* =
thus; *dadR^ishuH* = (and) looked; *tam vaanarashhabhaH* = (at) that best of Vanaras;
*aprameyam* = who is incomparable; *parvate* = (standing) on the mountain.

**अनुवादः**

Vidyadharas thus listened to the words of those great people and looked at the
incomparable Hanuma, best among Vanaras, standing on the mountain.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Hearing those words of those ascetics (_tapasvinām_), the Vidyādharas beheld on the mountain that immeasurable (_aprameyam_) bull among Vānaras (_vānararṣabham_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

दुधुवे च स रोमाणि चकम्पे चानलोपमः ।  
ननाद च महानादं सुमहानिव तोयदः ॥ ३२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

दुधुवे च स रोमाणि चकम्पे चानलोपमः ।  
ननाद च महानादं सुमहानिव तोयदः ॥ ३२ ॥
_________________
दुधुवे च स रोमाणि चकम्पे चाचलोपमः ।  
ननाद सुमहानादं स महानिव तोयदः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

दुधुवे च स रोमाणि चकम्पे चाचलोपमः ।
ननाद सुमहानादं सुमहानिव तोयदः ॥ ५-१-३२
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उस समय हनुमान् जी  अग्निके समान जान पड़ते थे । उन्होंने अपने शरीरको हिलाया और रोएँ झाड़े तथा महान् मेघके समान बड़े जोर-जोरसे गर्जना की ॥ ३२ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

आग आञ्जनेयनु महापर्वतदन्तॆ काणुत्तिद्दनु. अवन शरीरद रोम कोटिगळु निमिरिनिन्तिद्दुवु. मैयन्नॊम्मॆ ऒदरि, प्रळयकालद मेघवु गर्जिसुवन्तॆ ऒन्दु महानादवन्नु माडिदनु.॥32॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*32. saH* = He; *achalopamaH* = equal to a mountain; *dudhuve
cha* = shook; *romaaNi* = hair; *chakampe cha* = quivered (his body);
*nanaada* = (and ) sounded; *sumahaanaadam* = a great roar; *iva sumahaan
toyadaH* = like a vast cloud.

**अनुवादः**

He, who is equal to a mountain, shook his hair, quivered his body and let out a
great roar like from a vast cloud.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

He shook (_dudhuve_) his body-hairs (_romāṇi_), trembled like fire (_analopamaḥ_), and roared a mighty roar (_mahānādam_) like a vast rain-cloud (_sumahān iva toyadaḥ_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

आनुपूर्व्या च वृत्तं तल्लाङ्गूलं रोमभिश्चितम् ।  
उत्पतिष्यन् विचिक्षेप पक्षिराज इवोरगम् ॥ ३३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

आनुपूर्व्या च वृत्तं तल्लाङ्गूलं रोमभिश्चितम् ।  
उत्पतिष्यन् विचिक्षेप पक्षिराज इवोरगम् ॥ ३३ ॥
_________________
आनुपूर्व्येण वृत्तं च लाङ्गूलं रोमभिश्चितम् ।  
उत्पतिष्यन् विचिक्षेप पक्षिराज इवोरगम् ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

आनुपूर्व्येण वृत्तं च लाङ्गूलं लोमभिश्चितम् ।
उत्पतिष्यन् विचिक्षेप पक्षिराज इवोरगम् ॥५-१-३३
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

हनुमान् जी अब ऊपरको उछलना ही चाहते थे । उन्होंने क्रमशः गोलाकार मुड़ी तथा रोमावलियोंसे भरी हुई अपनी पूँछको उसी प्रकार आकाशमें फेंका, जैसे पक्षिराज गरुड़ सर्पको फेंकते हैं ॥ ३३ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

हारलु सन्नद्धवाद मारुतियु क्रमवन्ननुसरिसि सुत्तिकॊण्डिद्द, कूदलुगळिन्द तुम्बिहोगिद्द बालवन्नु गरुडनु हावन्नु ऎसॆयुवन्तॆ नीलाकाशदल्लि मेलक्कॆ चॆल्लिदनु.॥33॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*33. utpatishhyan* = About to fly; *vichikshepa* = (Hanuma)
jolted; *laaN^guulaM* = tail; *aanupuurvyeNa vR^ittam* = (rolled) in a circle from
top to bottom; *chitam* = covered; *lomabhiH* = with hair; *yathaa* = like;
*pakshi raajaH* = the king of birds (Garuda); *uragam* = (jolts) a snake.

**अनुवादः**

About to fly, Hanuma jolted his tail, which was rolled into a circle from top to
bottom, and which was covered with hair, just as Garuda, the king of birds would jolt a snake.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

About to spring up (_utpatiṣyan_), he brandished (_vicikṣepa_) that tail of his, rounded in due tapering order (_ānupūrvyā ca vṛttam_) and thick with hair (_romabhiś citam_), as the king of birds [Garuḍa] shakes a serpent (_pakṣirāja ivoragam_).
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

तस्य लाङ्गूलमाविद्धमतिवेगस्य पृष्ठतः ।  
ददृशे गरुडेनेव ह्रियमाणो महोरगः ॥ ३४ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

तस्य लाङ्गूलमाविद्धमतिवेगस्य पृष्ठतः ।  
ददृशे गरुडेनेव ह्रियमाणो महोरगः ॥ ३४ ॥
_________________
तस्य लाङ्गूलमाविद्धमात्तवेगस्य पृष्ठतः ।  
ददृशे गरुडेनेव ह्रियमाणो महोरगः ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

अत्यन्त वेगशाली हनुमान् जी के पीछे आकाशमें फैली हुई उनकी कुछ-कुछ मुड़ी हुई पूँछ गरुड़के द्वारा ले जाये जाते हुए महान् सर्पके समान दिखायी देती थी ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

आ मारुतियु तन्न बालवन्नु जोरागि कॊडहिदनु. अवन बॆन्न हिन्दॆ सम्बन्धवागिद्द महावेगवाद आ बालवु गरुडनिन्द सॆळॆयल्पडुत्तिद्द महासर्पदन्तॆ काणुत्तित्तु. ॥34॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*34. tasya aattavegasya* = receiving his vigor; *laaN^guulam* =
tail; *aaviddham* = curled; *pR^ishhThataH* = (at) his back; *dadR^ishe* = was
seen; *mahoragaH iva* = like a great serpent; *hriyamaaNaH* = being stolen;
*garudena* = by Garuda.

**अनुवादः**

Receiving his vigor, the tail curled at his back was seen like a great serpent being
stolen by Garuda.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Curled behind (_pṛṣṭhataḥ_) that hero of extreme impetuosity (_ativegasya_), his brandished (_āviddham_) tail appeared like a great serpent (_mahoragaḥ_) being carried off by Garuḍa.
</details>
</details>

बाहू संस्तम्भयामास महापरिघसन्निभौ ।  
आससाद कपिः कट्यां चरणौ सञ्चुकोच च ॥ ३५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

बाहू संस्तम्भयामास महापरिघसन्निभौ ।

बाहू संस्थम्भयामास महापरिघसन्निभौ ।  
ससाद च कपिः कट्यां चरणौ सञ्चुकोच च ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

बाहू संस्तंभयामास महापरिघसंनिभौ ।
ससाद च कपिः क्ट्यां चरणौ संचुकोच च ॥५-१-३५
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

उन्होंने अपनी विशाल परिघके समान भुजाओंको पर्वतपर जमाया । फिर ऊपरके सब अंगोंको इस तरह सिकोड़ लिया कि वे कटिकी सीमामें ही आ गये; साथ ही उन्होंने दोनों पैरोंको भी समेट लिया ॥ ३५ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

आ कपीश्वरनु महापरिघदन्तिरुव तन्नॆरडु तोळुगळन्नु बिगिगॊळिसिदनु. सॊण्टद मेलॆ शरीरद भारवन्निट्टनु. कालुगळन्नु बग्गिसिकॊण्डनु.॥35॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*35. kapiH* = Hanuma (lit. monkey); *saMstambhayaamaasa* =
supported;; *baahuu* = his arms; *mahaa parigha sannibhau* = which resembled huge
iron clubs; *sasaada* = crouched; *kaTyaam* = the waist; *cha saMchukocha* =
and contracted; *charaNau* = feet.

**अनुवादः**

Hanuma firmly supported his arms (on the mountain surface) which resembled huge iron
clubs, crouched the waist and contracted his feet.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

He planted firmly (_saṃstambhayām āsa_) his two arms, which resembled great iron bars (_mahāparighasaṃnibhau_); the monkey crouched at his waist (_āsasāda kaṭyām_) and contracted (_saṃcukoca_) his feet.
</details>
</details>

<details open><summary>विश्वास-प्रस्तुतिः</summary>

संहृत्य च भुजौ श्रीमांस्तथैव च शिरोधराम् ।  
तेजः सत्त्वं तथा वीर्यमाविवेश स वीर्यवान् ॥ ३६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः</summary>

<details><summary>मूलम्</summary>

संहृत्य च भुजौ श्रीमांस्तथैव च शिरोधराम् ।  
तेजः सत्त्वं तथा वीर्यमाविवेश स वीर्यवान् ॥ ३६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>मूलम् - VR</summary>

संहृत्य च भुजौ श्रीमांस्तथैव च शिरोधराम् ।
तेजः स्त्त्वं तथा वीर्यमाविवेश स वीर्यवान् ॥५-१-३६
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (हिन्दी)</summary>

तत्पश्चात् तेजस्वी और पराक्रमी हनुमान् जी ने अपनी दोनों भुजाओं और गर्दनको भी सिकोड़ लिया । इस समय उनमें तेज, बल और पराक्रम—सभीका आवेश हुआ ॥ ३६ ॥
</details>

<details><summary>अनुवाद (कन्नड)</summary>

शुभ लक्षण सम्पन्ननाद आ मारुतियु तन्न तोळुगळन्नु स्वल्प मडसिकॊण्डु कत्तन्नु चिक्कदु माडिकॊण्डनु. हारलु मुख्यवागि बेकागुव तेजस्सु, सत्त्व(बल)वन्नु, पराक्रमवन्नु तन्नल्लि तुम्बिकॊण्डनु.॥36॥
</details>

<details><summary>Murali Krishnamurthy Kopalle Murthy</summary>

**पदच्छेदः**

*36. saMhR^itya* = bending; *bhujau cha* = shoulders;
*tathaiva cha* = and also; *shirodharaam* = neck; *saH* = that (Hanuma);
*shrimaan* = (who is) glorious; *viiryavaan* = mighty; *aavivesha* =
increased; *tejaH* = energy; *sattvam* = strength; *tathaa* = and;
*viiryam* = courage.

**अनुवादः**

Bending shoulders and neck, that mighty and glorious Hanuma, increased his energy,
strength and courage.
</details>


<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Drawing in (_saṃhṛtya_) his two arms and likewise his neck (_śirodharām_), that glorious and valorous one summoned (_āviveśa_) his fiery energy, physical strength, and valor (_tejaḥ sattvaṃ tathā vīryam_).
</details>
</details>

<details><summary>सर्वाष् टीकाः - अवतारिका</summary>

<details><summary>English (Gemini 4, itihasa.io)</summary>

Holding his breath in his heart and planting his feet firmly with ears drawn back, Hanumān vows to the Vānaras to reach Laṅkā like Rāma’s arrow and return with Sītā or bind Rāvaṇa.
</details>
</details>

रुरोध हृदये प्राणानाकाशमवलोकयन् ॥ ३७ ॥
